<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

I think of two different numbers between $100$ and $200$ inclusive.
The highest common factor of my numbers is the same as the lowest common multiple of $8$ and $10$.

What two numbers could I have thought of?

</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Olly thinks of a positive whole number. When he divides $60$ by his number, the answer is also a whole number.
How many different numbers could Olly have thought of?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Olly thinks of a positive whole number. When he divides $60$ by his number, the answer is also a whole number.
How many different numbers could Olly have thought of?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 60}    &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Different numbers Olly thought of}        &= \text{Number of factors of 60} \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$12$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Four bells ring at intervals of $2, 8, 7$ and $11\ \text{seconds}$.

If they are all rung at the same time, how many seconds will pass before they all ring at the same time again?

You should show some justification for your answer. 

![papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i00.png](/assets/papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i00.png "papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i00.png")  ![papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i01.png](/assets/papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i01.png "papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i01.png")  ![papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i02.png](/assets/papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i02.png "papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i02.png")  ![papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i03.png](/assets/papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i03.png "papers/city-of-london-school/2020/sample-paper-1/s01-q22-i03.png")

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Four bells ring at intervals of $2, 8, 7$ and $11\ \text{seconds}$.
<emptyline>
If they are all rung at the same time, how many seconds will pass before they all ring at the same time again?
<emptyline>
You should show some justification for your answer. 
<emptyline>
%image%  %image%  %image%  %image%
</code></pre>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the biggest number that divides exactly into $60, 72$ and $84?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the biggest number that divides exactly into $60, 72$ and $84?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

To find the biggest number that divides exactly into $60$, $72$, and $84$, we need to find the highest common factor of the three numbers.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Prime factors of} \ 60 &= \bold {2 \times 2 \times 3} \times 5 \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 72 &= \bold {2 \times 2 \times 3} \times 2 \times 3 \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 84 &= \bold {2 \times 2 \times 3} \times 7
\end{aligned}
$

Highest common factor of $60$, $72$ and $84$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times 2 \times 3 \\\\
&= 12
\end{aligned}
$

The biggest number that divides exactly into $60$, $72$, and $84 = 12$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$12$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the biggest number that divides exactly into $28 , 84$ and $126?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the biggest number that divides exactly into $28 , 84$ and $126?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

To find the biggest number that divides exactly into $28$, $84$, and $126$, we need to find the highest common factor of the three numbers.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Prime factors of} \ 28 &= 2 \times \bold {2 \times 7} \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 84 &= 2 \times \bold {2} \times 3 \times \bold{7} \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 126 &= \bold {2} \times 3 \times \bold{7}
\end{aligned}
$

Highest common factor of $28$, $84$ and $126$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times 7 \\\\
&= 14
\end{aligned}
$

The biggest number that divides exactly into $28 , 84$ and $126 = 14$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$14$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

How many numbers less than $100$ are divisible by both $2$ and $5?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">How many numbers less than $100$ are divisible by both $2$ and $5?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

LCM of $2$ and $5 = 10$

Less than $100$ means numbers between $1$ to $99$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number divisible by both 2 and 5}         &= \dfrac {99} {10} \\\\ 
                                                &= 9 \dfrac {1} {10} \\\\
                                                &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Alternative solution

LCM of $2$ and $5 = 10$

Numbers less than 100 that are both divisible by $2$ and $5$ are numbers with $0$ at unit place.

$= 10, \ 20, \ 30, \ 40, \ 50, \ 60, \ 70, \ 80, \ 90$

Total count of numbers less than $100$ and both divisible by $2$ and $5 = 9$ 
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="formulacodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="formulacodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Formulas Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">LCM of $2$ and $5 = 10$
<emptyline>
Less than $100$ means numbers between $1$ to $99$.
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number divisible by both 2 and 5}         &= \dfrac {99} {10} \\\\ 
                                                &= 9 \dfrac {1} {10} \\\\
                                                &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="formulacodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="formulacodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Formulas Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Alternative solution
<emptyline>
LCM of $2$ and $5 = 10$
<emptyline>
Numbers less than 100 that are both divisible by $2$ and $5$ are numbers with $0$ at unit place.
<emptyline>
$= 10, \ 20, \ 30, \ 40, \ 50, \ 60, \ 70, \ 80, \ 90$
<emptyline>
Total count of numbers less than $100$ and both divisible by $2$ and $5 = 9$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answercodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the biggest number that divides into $14, 35,$ and $42?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the biggest number that divides into $14, 35,$ and $42?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

To find the biggest number that divides exactly into $14$, $35$, and $42$, we need to find the highest common factor of the three numbers.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Prime factors of} \ 14 &= 2 \times \bold{7} \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 35 &= 5 \times \bold{7} \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 42 &= 2 \times 3 \times \bold{7}
\end{aligned}
$

Highest common factor of $14$, $35$ and $42=7$

The biggest number that divides exactly into $14$, $35$, and $42=7$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$7$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the biggest number that divides into $16, 32,$ and $56?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the biggest number that divides into $16, 32,$ and $56?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

To find the biggest number that divides exactly into $16$, $32$, and $56$, we need to find the highest common factor of the three numbers.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Prime factors of} \ 16 &= 2 \times \bold{2 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 32 &= 2 \times 2 \times \bold{2 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
\text {Prime factors of} \ 56 &= \bold{2 \times 2 \times 2} \times 7
\end{aligned}
$

Highest common factor of $16$, $32$ and $56$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times 2 \times 2  \\\\
&= 8
\end{aligned}
$

The biggest number that divides exactly into $16$, $32$, and $56=8$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$8$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Two numbers $X$ and $Y$ (where $Y$ is bigger than $X$), have a Highest Common Factor of $X$. 
What is the Lowest Common Multiple of $X$ and $Y$?
- $Y$
- $X2$
- $Y2$
- $2Y$
- $XY$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Two numbers $X$ and $Y$ (where $Y$ is bigger than $X$), have a Highest Common Factor of $X$. 
What is the Lowest Common Multiple of $X$ and $Y$?
- $Y$
- $X2$
- $Y2$
- $2Y$
- $XY$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

X must be in included LCM.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{LCM}          &= X \times \dfrac{Y}{X} \\\\
                    &= \cancel{X} \times \dfrac{Y}{\cancel{X}} \\\\
                    &= Y
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="option" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Option**

<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$Y$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

When my age is divided by $2, 3, 4$ or $6$ there is always a remainder of $1$. 
But when divided by $7$ there is no remainder.
How old am I?

- $14$
- $49$ 
- $7$  
- $21$  
- $35$ 

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">When my age is divided by $2, 3, 4$ or $6$ there is always a remainder of $1$. 
But when divided by $7$ there is no remainder.
How old am I?
<emptyline>
- $14$
- $49$ 
- $7$  
- $21$  
- $35$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Lcm of 2,3,4,6 = 12

Let the number $= 12n + 1$

As per the question,

$12n + 1$ should be divisible by $7$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out the value of n which satisfies the condition above.

| n |  12n + 1               |  Divisible by 7 |
|:-:|:----------------------:|:---------------:|
|$1$| $12 \times 1 + 1 = 13$ |        No       |
|$2$| $12 \times 2 + 1 = 10$ |        Yes      |
|$3$| $12 \times 3 + 1 = 37$ |        No       |
|$4$| $12 \times 4 + 1 = 49$ |        Yes      |

A quick check shows that $n = 4$ meets all the criteria above.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number} &= 12 \times n + 1 \\\\
              &= 12 \times 4 + 1 \\\\
              &= 48 + 1 \\\\
              &= 49
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="option" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Option**

<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$49$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

At a party all the children were put into groups of $4$ to play a game. Two children had to sit out.

For another game they were put into groups of $5$. Two children had to sit out.

For the next game they were put into groups of $6$. No one had to sit out.

How many children were at the party?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">At a party all the children were put into groups of $4$ to play a game. Two children had to sit out.
<emptyline>
For another game they were put into groups of $5$. Two children had to sit out.
<emptyline>
For the next game they were put into groups of $6$. No one had to sit out.
<emptyline>
How many children were at the party?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Let the number of children $= n$

When the number is divided by $4$ number of children left $= 2$

When the number is divided by $5$ number of children left $= 2$

The number  $=$ LCM of $(4,5)n$ + 2 

We can easily infer their values by using trial and error. Let's find the number which meets the following critiera below.

- The number $= 20n + 2$.

- The number is a multiple of $6$.

|     n     |  value of the equation| Multiple of $6$  |
|:---------:|:---------------------:|:----------------:|
|   $1$     |       $22$            |        No        |
|   $2$     |       $42$            |        Yes       |
|   $3$     |       $62$            |        No        |
|   $4$     |       $82$            |        No        |
|   $5$     |       $102$           |        No        |

A quick check shows n $=2$ satisfies all the criteria above

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of children in the party} &= 20 \times 2 + 2 \\\\
								        &= 40 + 2 \\\\
								        &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$122$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Which number between $140$ and $170$ can be divided exactly by both $8$ and $9?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Which number between $140$ and $170$ can be divided exactly by both $8$ and $9?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

The LCM of $8$ and $9$ is $72$. 

Now let's find a number that is a multiple of the LCM and is between $140$ and $170$.

$72 \times 2 = 144$ is between $140$ and $170$.
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$144$

</div>
</div>

**Question**

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Which number between $50$ and $75$ is a multiple of both $3$ and $7?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the largest number less than $100$ which is a multiple of $2$, and of $3$, and also of $5?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the largest number less than $100$ which is a multiple of $2$, and of $3$, and also of $5?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

As per the question,

Let's first find the LCM of the three numbers. There are no common factors, so the LCM would be 

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{LCM}              &= 2 \times 3 \times 5 \\\\
                        &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Now let's find the largest number that is a multiple of the LCM and is less than $100$.

$
\begin{aligned}
30 \times 2 &= 60   && \text{less than 100} \\\\
30 \times 3 &= 90   && \text{less than 100} \\\\
30 \times 4 &= 120  && \text{greater than 100}
\end{aligned}
$

Largest number $= 90$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="formulacodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Formulas Raw</strong>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>

<emptyline>
$

                        &= 30

$

<emptyline>

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

The *facsum* of a number is the sum of all its factors.

For example, the *facsum* of $6$ is $12$ because $1 + 2 + 3 + 6 = 12$. Work out the *facsum* of $20$.

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">The *facsum* of a number is the sum of all its factors.
<emptyline>
For example, the *facsum* of $6$ is $12$ because $1 + 2 + 3 + 6 = 12$. Work out the *facsum* of $20$.
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

All the factors of $20 =  1, 2, 4, 5, 10, 20$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fascum of 20 }   &= 1 + 2 + 4 + 5 + 10 + 20 \\\\
                        &= 42
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$42$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="formulacodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="formulacodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Formulas Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">All the factors of $20 =  1, 2, 4, 5, 10, 20$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fascum of 20 }   &= 1 + 2 + 4 + 5 + 10 + 20 \\\\
                        &= 42
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

A red light flashes four times a minute and a green light flashes six times a minute. If both lights start flashing at the same instant, how many times do they flash at the same instant in one hour including the first and the last time?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">A red light flashes four times a minute and a green light flashes six times a minute. If both lights start flashing at the same instant, how many times do they flash at the same instant in one hour including the first and the last time?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Interval at which red light flashes}         &= \dfrac {60} {4} \\\\
                                                   &= \dfrac {15 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                                   &= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                                   &= 15 \ \text{s} \\\\
\text{Interval at which blue light flashes}        &= \dfrac {60} {6} \\\\
                                                   &= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {6} \\\\
                                                   &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                                   &= 10 \ \text{s}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Interval at which both will flash}        &= \text{LCM of 15 and 10} \\\\
                                                &= 30 \ \text{s}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of times they flash together}     &= \dfrac {60 \times 60} {30} + 1 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 2 \times 60} {30} + 1 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 2 \times 60} {\cancel {30}} + 1 \\\\
                                                &= 2 \times 60 + 1 \\\\
                                                &= 120 + 1 \\\\
                                                &= 121
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$121$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>

                                                   &= \dfrac {15 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                                   &= 15 \ \text{s} \\\\
\text{Interval at which blue light flashes}        &= \dfrac {60} {6} \\\\
                                                   &= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {6} \\\\
                                                   &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                                                   &= 10 \ \text{s}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

 At Frantic Junction, trains arrive at regular intervals on
three different lines. On the line from Aytown, trains arrive every
$15 \ \text{minutes}$. On the line from Beeville, they arrive every
$12 \ \text{minutes}$ and from Ceeford they arrive every $8 \ \text
{minutes}$.  At $10{:}00 \ \text{am}$ a train from each town arrives at Frantic
Junction.

What is the next time that three trains will arrive together?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

How many whole numbers less than $100$ **cannot** be divided exactly by $4$ or by $5?$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">How many whole numbers less than $100$ **cannot** be divided exactly by $4$ or by $5?$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

It is easier to calculate the numbers divisible by $4$ and $5$ instead of calculating the numbers which are **NOT** divisible.

Multiples of $4 = 100 \div 4 = 25$

Multiples of $5 = 100 \div 5 = 20$

The above calculations will count the numbers which are multiples of both $4$ and $5$ **twice**, so we need to account for that. The LCM of $4$ and $5$ is $20$.

Multiples of $20 = 100 \div 20 = 5$

So finally, let's calculate the numbers which cannot be exactly divided by $4$  and $5$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 100 - (25 + 20 - 5) \\\\
&= 100 - 40 \\\\
&= 60
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$

&= 100 - 40 \\\\
&= 60
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

A large number of buses stop at the bus stop at the end of Old Hall Lane. 
The $42$ bus stops there every $6 \ \text{minutes}$, the $43$ bus stops there every $8 \ \text{minutes}$ and the $45$ bus stops every $15 \ \text{minutes}$. 
If all the buses stop at the Old Hall Lane stop at $4.00 \ \text{pm}$, 
write down the total number of buses that will call at the stop **between** $4.20$ and $4.50 \ \text{pm}$.

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">A large number of buses stop at the bus stop at the end of Old Hall Lane. 
The $42$ bus stops there every $6 \ \text{minutes}$, the $43$ bus stops there every $8 \ \text{minutes}$ and the $45$ bus stops every $15 \ \text{minutes}$. 
If all the buses stop at the Old Hall Lane stop at $4.00 \ \text{pm}$, 
write down the total number of buses that will call at the stop **between** $4.20$ and $4.50 \ \text{pm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

As per the question all buses start at = $4.00 \ \text{pm}$

Bus $42$ stops every $6$  minutes : use multiples of  $6$

Let's workout the total buses that stop between $4.20$ and $4.50$ 

Bus $42:$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
\text {Starts} &: 4:06 & 4:12 & 4:18\\
\text {Between}&: 4:24 & 4:30 & 4:36 & 4:42 & 4:48 \\
\end{array}
$

No. of Buses $= 5$ 

Bus $43$ stops every $8$ minutes :use multiples of $8$

Bus $43:$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
\text {Starts} &: 4:08 & 4:16 \\
\text {Between}&: 4:24 & 4:32 & 4:40 & 4:48 \\
\end{array}
$

No. of Buses $= 4$ 

Bus $45$ stops every $15$ minutes :use multiples of $15$

Bus $45:$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
\text {Starts} &: 4:00 & 4:15\\
\text {Between}&: 4:30 & 4:45 \\
\end{array}
$

No. of Buses $= 2$ 

$5 + 4 + 2 =11$ buses stop between $4:20 \ \text{pm}$ and $4:50 \ \text{pm}$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>

<emptyline>
Bus $42$ stops every $6$  minutes : use multiples of  $6$

$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

</code></pre>

</div>
